Draft

4. Conclusions 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 4, "bbox_points": [85.08003234863281, 100.87147521972656, 529.4054565429688, 111.23372650146484]}]
- 
Hydrogen sulphide is an undesirable product from anaerobic digestion, produced when sulfate is present 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [99.24005889892578, 124.32330322265625, 529.4059448242188, 134.27369689941406]}]
in the influent. Until recently, there was no practical strategy based on varying operational conditions used 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [99.24005889892578, 147.24334716796875, 529.4052734375, 157.19374084472656]}]
to avoid sulfate reduction. Taking into account thermodynamic and kinetic parameters, this process is 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [99.24005889892578, 170.2833251953125, 529.4054565429688, 180.95729064941406]}]
more favourable than methanogenesis. Part of H2S is transferr to biogas, causing corrosion problems 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [99.23969268798828, 193.32342529296875, 529.405517578125, 203.9972686767578]}]
during methane combustion due to SOx compounds formation. Therefore, the most efective way to avoid 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 9, "bbox_points": [99.23954010009766, 216.243408203125, 302.60540771484375, 226.91725158691406]}]
the negative effects caused by H2S is to remove it. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [85.08003234863281, 238.87159729003906, 529.4058227539062, 249.2338409423828]}]
- 
Sulfate reduction causes a double negative effect on methane production during the anaerobic process. On 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 11, "bbox_points": [99.2400894165039, 262.32342529296875, 529.405517578125, 272.2738342285156]}]
one hand, sulfate reduction consumes part of COD then; less organic matter is available for methanogens. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [99.2400894165039, 285.2434387207031, 529.4058227539062, 295.91729736328125]}]
On the other hand, the H2S generated inhibits the activity of methanogens. This last effect could be 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 13, "bbox_points": [99.24036407470703, 308.28350830078125, 529.4055786132812, 318.95733642578125]}]
minimized by oxidizing H2S to elemental sulphur under microaerobic conditions inside the anaerobic 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 14, "bbox_points": [99.24027252197266, 331.3235168457031, 483.9259033203125, 341.27392578125]}]
reactor with a decrease in caloric value largely due to the increase of nitrogen present in the air. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 15, "bbox_points": [85.08061218261719, 353.8316955566406, 529.40576171875, 364.9173583984375]}]
- 
To reduce oxygen demand it is necessary to remove the H2S present in the effluent of the anaerobic 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 16, "bbox_points": [99.2397232055664, 377.2835388183594, 529.4055786132812, 387.23394775390625]}]
digester. Oxidation of hydrogen sulphide is accieved in biofilm reactors using oxygen or nitrate 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 17, "bbox_points": [99.2397232055664, 400.3235778808594, 529.4053955078125, 410.27398681640625]}]
(autotrophic denitrification), autotrophic denitrification being the most advisable option when nitrogen 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 18, "bbox_points": [99.2397232055664, 423.2436218261719, 182.0048828125, 433.19403076171875]}]
removal is required. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 19, "bbox_points": [248.56744384765625, 324.0377197265625, 370.02056884765625, 452.7870178222656]}]
Draft


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 20, "bbox_points": [85.07966613769531, 445.87176513671875, 529.404541015625, 456.2340393066406]}]
- 
Currently, biofilms using chemolithotrophic sulphide oxidizing bacteria is recommended, due to a higher 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 21, "bbox_points": [99.23868560791016, 469.20367431640625, 425.0036926269531, 479.1540832519531]}]
sulphide loading, simpler nutritional requirements and higher sulphide tolerance. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 22, "bbox_points": [85.07801818847656, 491.8318176269531, 529.4030151367188, 502.194091796875]}]

Biofilm systems used for sulphide removal, utilizing different kinds of electron acceptors (nitrate, nitrite, 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 23, "bbox_points": [99.23709869384766, 515.28369140625, 529.4026489257812, 525.2340698242188]}]
oxygen) have been proposed, highlighting an important potential for their use at an industrial scale. The

sulphide removal efficiencies in these systems were most of the time superior to 90%.

- 
Microbial fuel cell is a new technology used in the removal of sulphide at the laboratory scale and has

generating power between 40 W m-2 and 740 W m-2. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 28, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 28, "bbox_points": [85.08042907714844, 629.8318481445312, 529.4058227539062, 640.194091796875]}]
